830	## docs: renumber todo.md references (0.48.4)
831	
832	Compact `notes/todo.md`'s `# References` to a contiguous
833	`[1]..[N]` in first-citation-appearance order — scan the
834	`## Todo` items, then the `## Done` items, in document order,
835	and assign `[1]`, `[2]`, … to refs as their first `[[N]]`
836	citation appears. The 0.48.3 retrofits left `todo.md` with a
837	sparse namespace (gaps from pruned entries, numbers running
838	into the 90s); the renumber re-packs it so the next new ref
839	is `[N+1]`, not `[96]`.
840	
841	- **File-local.** Only `todo.md`'s `[[N]]` citations and
842	  `[N]:` definition lines move; every `[N]:` *target* and
843	  every other note file is untouched (ref numbers are
844	  file-local slots — `done.md` keeps its own numbering).
845	- **First-citation order, not definition order.** The number
846	  a reader meets first is `[1]`; following a citation walks
847	  *down* the `# References` list, never up. (Definition order
848	  would scatter the reading sequence.)
849	- **Cosmetic: multi-ref groups sorted ascending.** A citation
850	  cluster renders `[[9]],[[13]],[[14]]`, not the order the
851	  refs happened to land in.
852	
853	### Why renumber todo.md only
854	
855	- `todo.md` is the live churn surface — entries land and get
856	  pruned every cycle, so its namespace fragments fastest and
857	  benefits most from a re-pack. `chores-NN.md` / `done.md`
858	  are append-mostly; their gaps are rare and not worth a
859	  rewrite pass.
860	- A renumber is a whole-file rewrite of citations + defs;
861	  doing it opportunistically (when the namespace has drifted
862	  enough to annoy) beats a standing "keep it dense" rule that
863	  would force a rewrite on every prune.
864

105	`todo.md`'s namespace fragments as entries land and get pruned, so
106	its `# References` is **periodically re-packed** to a contiguous
107	`[1]..[N]` in first-citation-appearance order (scan `## Todo` then
108	`## Done` in document order). This is a file-local rewrite — only
109	`todo.md`'s `[[N]]` citations and `[N]:` definitions move; every
110	target and sibling file is untouched. Do it opportunistically
111	(when the gaps annoy), not on every prune. `chores-NN.md` /

      850: Properties of the renumber:
      851: 
      852: - **File-local.** Only the file's own `[[N]]` citations and
      853:   `[N]:` definition lines move; every `[N]:` *target* and every

+## docs: renumber todo.md + chores-09 refs (0.48.4)
+
+Compact two files' `# References` to a contiguous `[1]..[N]` in
+first-citation-appearance order — walk the file's prose in
+document order (`todo.md`: `## Todo` items then `## Done` items;
+`chores-09.md`: top to bottom) and assign `[1]`, `[2]`, … as
+each ref's first `[[N]]` citation appears.
+
+- **`todo.md`** — the 0.48.2/0.48.3 retrofits and routine entry
+  churn left a sparse namespace (gaps from pruned entries,
+  numbers into the 90s); the re-pack makes the next new ref
+  `[N+1]`, not `[96]`.
+- **`chores-09.md`** — the 0.48.2/0.48.3 bulk retrofit allocated
+  slots in *conversion* order, not document order: `[1]` was the
+  first section converted (the 0.48.1 section, near the bottom),
+  `[2]..[22]` the historical sections top-to-bottom, `[24]` the
+  chores-07 design ref (first cited near the top). The re-pack
+  puts the numbers back in reading order — `[1]` is the topmost
+  citation.
+
+Properties of the renumber:
+
+- **File-local.** Only the file's own `[[N]]` citations and
+  `[N]:` definition lines move; every `[N]:` *target* and every
+  other note file is untouched (ref numbers are file-local
+  slots — `done.md` keeps its own numbering).
+- **First-citation order, not definition order.** The number a
+  reader meets first is `[1]`; following a citation walks *down*
+  the `# References` list, never up.
+- **Code spans are not citations.** A `[[N]]` inside a `` ` ``
+  span (e.g. this section's `[[9]],[[13]],[[14]]` example) is a
+  literal token, not a reference — the script masks fenced and
+  inline code before scanning.
+- **Cosmetic: multi-ref groups sorted ascending** — a cluster is
+  written `[[9]],[[13]],[[14]]`, not in the order the refs
+  happened to land.
+
+### When to re-pack a file's references
+
+- A renumber is a whole-file rewrite of citations + defs — do it
+  opportunistically (when the namespace has drifted enough to
+  annoy) rather than under a standing "keep it dense" rule that
+  would force a rewrite on every prune.
+- `todo.md` is the live churn surface (entries land and get
+  pruned every cycle) so it fragments fastest and is the usual
+  candidate. `chores-NN.md` / `done.md` are append-mostly; their
+  numbering only drifts out of reading order after an unusual
+  event like the 0.48.2/0.48.3 retrofit — which is exactly why
+  `chores-09` got re-packed here and the other chores files
+  don't need it.
